'/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/arena/SKILL.md'\",\"max_output_tokens\":12000},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"6a26aa\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000005042,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":1884,\"output\":\"---\\nname: arena\\ndescription: \\\"Spawn N parallel candidates at the same task, pick a base, graft the strongest parts of the losers into it. Use for /arena, 'arena this', 'throw it in the arena', or when one attempt at a non-trivial artifact would lock in the wrong shape.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Arena\\n\\nRead [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment and integration](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Use only exposed, authorized operations. Read each applied principle leaf in full before dependent work. Missing required reads block that work. Invocation aliases are guidance, not proof of registered host commands.\\n\\nIndependent candidates and a fresh independent judge require observed worker operations and inspected artifacts. If unavailable, produce authorized sequential alternatives and parent comparison only as reduced capability. Do not claim independence, background execution, model diversity or a full arena pass. If independence is required, leave that gate blocked.\\n\\nFan out N parallel attempts at the same task. Read every candidate end to end. Pick the strongest as the base. Graft the best ideas from the others into it. Verify the synthesized result.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen an exposed todo list with one entry per phase before launching anything. Without one, use an authorized local Markdown checklist and disclose the reduced binding.\\n\\n1. Frame\\n2. Fan out\\n3. Cross-judge\\n4. Pick\\n5. Graft\\n6. Verify\\n\\n## Phase A: Frame\\n\\nThe N candidates will receive the same prompt, so the prompt is the contract.\\n\\n1. State the artifact each candidate is producing.\\n2. Derive the rubric. State what success looks like for *this* task, then turn it into 3-6 concrete gradeable criteria. The rubric is the picker's tool in Phase D. Candidates only see the task.\\n3. Pick the runners. Use an explicitly supplied, authorized `arena runners` preference if available. Otherwise request two inherited workers, one per seat. The source defaults used two specific model families. Their source-host slugs are not portable availability evidence. Select an explicit model or effort only from current exposed documentation or observed enumeration under the model-role contract. An `auto` or `inherit-parent` entry means omit explicit model selection. On rejection, record it and disclose a supported same-family alternative when available, otherwise an authorized inherited alternative. If the exact model is required, block instead. Record requested and observed model and effort separately, using unknown when unobserved. More seats can cover multiple design directions. Same model N times is appropriate when work is generation-bound rather than judgment-sensitive. Unknown models leave diversity unverified.\\n4. Assign output paths. Each candidate writes to its own authorized location, a git worktree where supported, otherwise a distinct local candidate directory. Follow [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Confirm isolation and write permissions before dispatch. Record immutable assignment identity, input SHA, owner, role and evidence links under the assignment contract. Workers never integrate shared files. Worktree creation and persistence require observed authorized capabilities.\\n\\n## Phase B: Fan out\\n\\nDispatch all N independent candidates together through the exposed worker operation, using observed background execution when available. Give each the same task, shared grounding path, its isolated output path, and instructions to produce both the artifact and a short rationale. Withhold the rubric from candidates. Inspect terminal results and artifact paths before accepting completion; a wait notification alone is insufficient.\\n\\nEach rationale names the alternatives the candidate considered and what it rejected.\\n\\nIf a candidate fails to produce output, proceed with N-1 and note the dropout in the synthesis record. One survivor means incomplete competition, even if its artifact is useful. Zero survivors blocks synthesis. Never integrate partial or cancelled artifacts as completed candidates.\\n\\n## Phase C: Cross-judge\\n\\nAfter all Phase B candidates reach terminal state and their accepted artifacts are inspected, choose one model from an explicitly supplied, authorized `arena cross-judge pool` preference. Without that preference, use an inherited worker or a supported explicit selection under the model-role contract. The source pool named the same two families as the runners; it does not establish portable model availability. Prefer a different observed model family from the parent's when available. Disclose rejected choices and supported fallbacks, or block if the exact choice is required.\\n\\nSpawn one fresh readonly judge worker. It sees the rubric and completed candidates by path label, scores each criterion, and recommends a base with rationale. It runs in parallel with the parent's reading in Phase D when observed background execution permits it, never with candidates still writing. The judge has no candidate write ownership. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified. Unavailable fresh judging remains a reduced or blocked gate, never an invented independent verdict.\\n\\n## Phase D: Pick a base\\n\\nRead every candidate end to end before picking.\\n\\nScore each candidate against the rubric criterion by criterion, not on holistic feel. Compare against the cross-judge. Agreement on the base confirms the pick. Disagreement means one of you is biased or the rubric was ambiguous. Read both rationales before deciding.\\n\\nPick the base on which candidate a future maintainer can extend most easily without breaking invariants. Prefer the cleaner boundary or smaller API when two feel tied, per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md).\\n\\nRecord the pick and the reason in a short synthesis note alongside the base artifact, including the cross-judge's verdict.\\n\\n## Phase E: Graft\\n\\nWalk each losing candidate once more and identify what is worth porting into the base. The signal is usually one or two things per candidate, not most of it.\\n\\nFold each graft in by hand, per [redesign-from-first-principles](../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md). Don't paste mechanically. The result has to remain coherent under one mental model.\\n\\nRecord what was grafted, from which candidate, and what was rejected and why.\\n\\nWhen N candidates converge on the same shape, that is a strong agreement signal. Note the convergence in the record and deliver the consensus shape within authorized scope. No graft is needed. When N candidates wildly diverge, Phase A was under-specified. Reframe and re-run rather than averaging the divergence.\\n\\n## Phase F: Verify\\n\\nThe synthesized artifact has to hold up under the same scrutiny as any other output, per [prove-it-works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\\n\\nIf verification surfaces a problem the arena did not catch, either Phase A was wrong (re-frame and re-run) or one candidate caught it and you missed the graft (go back to Phase E). Don't paper over.\\n\\nApply [unslop](../unslop/SKILL.md) to the synthesis prose. External publication, commits, messages and setup require their own authorization.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\nOne synthesized artifact. One short synthesis note alongside, naming the base, the grafts and their source candidates, the rejections, the dropouts if any, and the verification result.\\n\"}}},{\"path\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md\",\"tool\":{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md'\",\"max_output_tokens\":12000},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"1b438d\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000027708,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":837,\"output\":\"---\\nname: principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Encode the rule as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Encode Lessons in Structure\\n\\nEncode recurring fixes in mechanisms (tools, code, metadata, automation) instead of textual instructions. Every error, human correction, and unexpected outcome is a learning signal. Capture it, route it, and close the loop.\\n\\n**Why:** Textual instructions are easy to miss. They require the reader to notice, remember, and comply. Structural mechanisms (lint rules, metadata flags, runtime checks, automation scripts) enforce the rule without cooperation.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\nWhen you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time:\\n1. Ask: can this be a lint rule, a metadata flag, a runtime check, or a script?\\n2. If yes, encode it. Delete the instruction\\n3. If no (requires judgment), make the instruction more prominent and add an example of the failure mode\\n\\n**Pick the strongest mechanism.** When more than one mechanism would work, choose the strongest the situation allows (an unrepresentable state that cannot compile, then a lint or banned API that fails CI, then a canonical helper, then a runtime check), because agents copy whatever the surrounding code already does and a weaker guard becomes the next template.\\n\\n**Corollary:** If the fix is structural, only use the structural fix. The instruction is the symptom.\\n\\n**Feedback loop:**\\n- **Capture every correction.** When the human intervenes or tests fail, decide if it's a one-off or a pattern.\\n- **Route to the right layer.** One-off -> brain note. Recurring fix -> skill or lint rule. Systemic issue -> principle.\\n- **Close the loop.** Don't just record. Apply now or create a concrete todo.\\n\\n**Anti-patterns:**\\n- Acknowledging without recording (\\\"I'll keep that in mind\\\" does not persist)\\n- Recording without routing (a brain note about a lint rule that should exist is wasted unless the lint rule gets implemented)\\n- Fixing without generalizing (fixing one instance while leaving the recurring pattern intact)\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for reads, writes, execution and durable persistence. Apply mechanisms within the authorized task. This principle does not authorize installation, global settings or external actions. Treat a brain note as an authorized durable record when persistence is available; claim it saved only after write and readback. If persistence is unavailable, state that limit and include the concrete todo in the current response.\\n\\nIf writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal that names the check, its integration point and the remaining implementation or verification work. If the rule or source needed to select a mechanism is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep the proposal provisional. Delete a redundant instruction only once the structural fix enforces its rule. Preserve judgment-dependent guidance with a prominent failure example.\\n\\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it. Claim enforcement, implementation or verification only for observed operations.\\n\"}}},{\"path\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/workflow-ownership.md\",\"tool\":{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/workflow-ownership.md'\",\"max_output_tokens\":16000},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"c09e4c\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000004458,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":317,\"output\":\"# Workflow ownership\\n\\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\\n\\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\\n\\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\\n\\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\\n\"}}},{\"path\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/host-capabilities.md\",\"tool\":{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/host-capabilities.md'\",\"max_output_tokens\":11000},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"2ff0f9\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.0000065,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":1789,\"output\":\"# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n\"}}},{\"path\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/model-roles.md\",\"tool\":{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/model-roles.md'\",\"max_output_tokens\":11000},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"adbd01\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000006042,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":718,\"output\":\"# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n\"}}}],\"cli\":{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat principle-minimize-reader-load\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers between question and answer, and hidden state in the reader's head; collapse one-caller wrappers and shrink mutable scope.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Minimize Reader Load\\n\\nMaintainability is the work a reader must do to understand code. Track two axes:\\n1. **Layers to trace.** How many indirections sit between the question and the answer.\\n2. **State to hold.** How much hidden or mutable context the reader must keep in their head.\\n\\n**Why:** Code is read far more than it is written. LOC, cyclomatic complexity, and \\\"clean architecture\\\" are proxies. Reader load is the thing that matters. The two axes are independent. A flat file with 50 globals can be as hard to reason about as a 6-layer adapter stack. Guard both. This is the human analog of [Guard the Context Window](../principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md). Working memory is finite for readers too.\\n\\n**The pattern:**\\n- **Collapse layers** that cost more than they save: wrappers with one caller, adapters with no second implementation, speculative indirection that was never needed. Inline them.\\n- **Make adjacent layers change the abstraction.** A layer that repeats the same methods and arguments adds reader load without compression. Collapse pass-through layers.\\n- **Demand interface compression.** A broad interface that hides little complexity makes readers learn both the surface and the implementation. Prefer boundaries that hide meaningful decisions.\\n- **Shrink state scope:** prefer pure functions (returns over mutations), locals over fields, fields over module state, and module state over globals. Derive instead of sync.\\n- **Name the invariant at the boundary,** not in every consumer, so the reader learns it once.\\n- Before adding a layer or a piece of state, ask: does this reduce reader load somewhere else by at least as much?\\n\\n**The test:** Can a new reader answer \\\"where does X come from?\\\" and \\\"what can change X?\\\" in under 30 seconds? If not, cut layers or cut state.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to discover available read, write and execution tools. Inspect the concrete path, its callers and mutable state before deciding which layers to collapse. Make edits only within the authorized task and available write permissions. Run appropriate checks only through observed execution tools.\\n\\nWhen source is readable but writes or execution are denied or unavailable, give a source-grounded proposal and identify the edits or checks you could not perform. Without source or sufficient caller and state evidence, report the missing evidence and stop concrete refactoring claims. Do not claim a measured reader time without observation. Preserve invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Separate Before Serializing Shared State\\n\\nWhen concurrent actors might share mutable state, first ask whether they need the same mutable object. If not, eliminate the sharing. When sharing is real, enforce serialization structurally: lockfiles, sequential phases, exclusive ownership. Instructions and conventions are not concurrency control.\\n\\n**Why:** Concurrent writes to shared state create race conditions that are intermittent, hard to reproduce, and expensive to debug.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\n1. **Identify shared mutable state** (files both read and write, branches both push to, APIs both define and consume).\\n2. **Default: eliminate the shared write target.** Ask: do these actors need one canonical object, or are they publishing independent facts? Give each actor its own owned file, key, branch, or state directory, and merge only at the read/reporting boundary. Two workers writing their own `lastX` field into one `state.json` is still shared mutation. `indexer-state.json` + `metrics-state.json` is not.\\n3. **Only when one shared write target is a real invariant, serialize access structurally** (lockfiles, sequential phases, single-writer actor, or atomic compare-and-swap). Treat \\\"we need a lock\\\" as a design smell to check, not as the default answer.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nBefore dispatching writers or changing shared state, check [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) and follow the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Assign distinct owned paths before writes start. Isolation does not grant permission to spawn workers, write files or perform external actions.\\n\\nIf execution or writes are unavailable or denied, state the missing requirement and provide the isolation design without claiming implementation. Authorized sequential work is reduced capability. A design or sequential run does not prove independent workers or prevention of concurrent races.\\n---\\nname: typescript-best-practices\\ndescription: TypeScript best practices. Use when reading or editing any .ts or .tsx file.\\npaths: [\\\"**/*.ts\\\", \\\"**/*.tsx\\\"]\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# TypeScript best practices\\n\\nApply the **type-system-discipline** principle skill first.\\n\\n| Rule | Summary |\\n|------|---------|\\n| Discriminated unions | Model variants with a `kind` literal discriminant so impossible states can't be represented. No optional-field bags. |\\n| Branded types | Brand primitives with `& { readonly __brand: \\\"X\\\" }` so they can't be mixed up. Validate once at the boundary. |\\n| Constructive modeling | Build the shape so the illegal value can't be constructed. `[T, ...T[]]` for non-empty, `[T, T][]` for even length, `start` plus `duration` for a range. Not a runtime guard, not a wish for refinement types. |\\n| Simplest total type | Keep `T[]` while every operation on it stays total. Strengthen to `NonEmpty<T>` only where the loose type forces `!`, a cast, or a \\\"should never happen\\\" throw. |\\n| `unknown` over `any` | External data is `unknown`. |\\n| Schemas before guards | Before hand-writing a property-by-property type guard, use the repository's runtime schema library and infer the type from the schema, such as `z.infer`. |\\n| No `as` casts | Every `as` is a runtime crash waiting. Cast only after validation. |\\n| Narrowing hierarchy | Discriminant switch > `in` operator > `typeof`/`instanceof` > user-defined type guard > `as`. |\\n| Type guards | Must verify the claim. A lying guard is worse than `as` because the bug hides behind a name that says it's safe. Name them `isX` or `hasX`. |\\n| Exhaustiveness | Inline `const _exhaustive: never = x;` in default arms so the compiler errors when a new variant is added. |\\n| `satisfies` over `as` | Validates the value without widening literal types. |\\n| Boundary validation | Parse where data crosses in, into a named domain type. `Record<string, unknown>` (however spelled) stops at that parse. Trust types inside. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill. |\\n| Schema-derived types | Reach for `Pick`/`Omit`/`Parameters`/`ReturnType`/`Awaited`/`typeof` before declaring a new interface. |\\n| Object args | Pass objects, not positional, so argument order is self-documenting. Skip on hot paths (per-frame render, tokenizers, parsers). |\\n| Real tests | Don't mock what you can run. Prefer the framework's real test primitives with leak/disposable checks, and verify UI in a running build. Mock only what you can't run locally. |\\n| Structured telemetry | Prefer structured logger diagnostics with enough context to debug from an id. No `console.log` in shipped code. |\\n\\nExamples: `references/patterns.md`.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for the current session. Read the [type-system-discipline](../principle-type-system-discipline/SKILL.md) principle first, and the [boundary-discipline](../principle-boundary-discipline/SKILL.md) principle for boundary validation. Use the complete [patterns reference](references/patterns.md). If a dependency cannot be read, name it and label the review partial.\\n\\nApply these practices to supplied code or readable repository files. Saved edits require available write tools and authorization for the affected paths. If writes are denied or unavailable, return a proposed refactor in the conversation and state that it is unsaved. If no TypeScript input can be read or supplied, report the missing input before proceeding.\\n\\nRun the repository's checks only with available execution tools and authorized commands. Reuse its installed runtime schema library; do not install a dependency for one guard. Report compiler, test, and running UI checks only after observing their results. If execution is denied or unavailable, identify the checks that remain unperformed. Retain the source metadata without assuming that every host enforces paths or disable-model-invocation.\\n# TypeScript patterns\\n\\nCode examples for each rule in `SKILL.md`. The underlying principles are language-agnostic. See the **type-system-discipline** and **boundary-discipline** principle skills.\\n\\n## Branded types\\n\\nBrand primitives so they can't be mixed up. Validate once at the boundary. Downstream code trusts the type.\\n\\n```ts\\ntype AgentId = string & { readonly __brand: \\\"AgentId\\\" };\\n\\nfunction parseAgentId(input: string): AgentId {\\n  if (!isUUID(input)) throw new Error(`Invalid agent id: ${input}`);\\n  return input as AgentId;\\n}\\n\\nfunction focusAgent(id: AgentId): void {\\n  /* input is trusted */\\n}\\n```\\n\\nMatch the `readonly __brand: 'X'` shape. Don't invent a new convention.\\n\\n## Discriminated unions\\n\\nModel variants with a literal discriminant. Every variant shares the field name and each variant's value is unique, so impossible combos can't be represented.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Boolean + optionals lets contradictory states exist.\\ntype DiffState = { loading: boolean; diff?: GitDiff; error?: string };\\n\\n// Do. Only valid states exist.\\ntype DiffState =\\n  | { kind: \\\"loading\\\" }\\n  | { kind: \\\"ready\\\"; diff: GitDiff }\\n  | { kind: \\\"error\\\"; error: string };\\n```\\n\\nPick one discriminant name (`kind`, `type`, `tag`) and stick to it.\\n\\n## Constructive modeling\\n\\nBuild the type from parts that are all legal instead of restricting a loose type with runtime checks.\\n\\nNon-empty, via a variadic tuple:\\n\\n```ts\\ntype NonEmpty<T> = [T, ...T[]];\\n\\n// Don't: T[] plus a length check every caller must repeat\\nfunction pickWinner(entries: string[]): string {\\n  if (entries.length === 0) throw new Error(\\\"no entries\\\");\\n  return entries[Math.floor(Math.random() * entries.length)];\\n}\\n\\n// Do: an empty value of the type can't exist\\nfunction pickWinner(entries: NonEmpty<string>): string {\\n  return entries[Math.floor(Math.random() * entries.length)];\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWhere a plain `T[]` arrives, narrow once with a guard. The fact then travels in the type:\\n\\n```ts\\nconst isNonEmpty = <T>(arr: T[]): arr is NonEmpty<T> => arr.length > 0;\\n```\\n\\nEven length, as pairs:\\n\\n```ts\\ntype Pairs<T> = [T, T][];\\n```\\n\\nA time range, as start plus duration:\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't: a comment holds the invariant\\ntype TimeRange = { start: Date; end: Date }; // start <= end\\n\\n// Do: a negative range can't be written; derive end when needed\\ntype TimeRange = { start: Date; durationMs: number };\\n```\\n\\nKeep `durationMs` a plain number. Brand it (per Branded types) only if a raw number could be passed where a duration is expected, not by reflex. Pick the representation that makes the bad state unconstructable, then expose the reading you need on top (`pairs.flat()`, a `rangeEnd()` helper).\\n\\n## Simplest total type\\n\\nDon't strengthen everything. Keep `T[]` when every operation on it is total:\\n\\n```ts\\nconst sum = (xs: number[]) => xs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0); // [] is 0, fine\\n```\\n\\nStrengthen when the loose type forces a lie at a use site. The tells are `!`, `arr[0] as T`, and a \\\"should never happen\\\" throw:\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't: partiality smuggled past the compiler\\nfunction newestSession(sessions: Session[]): Session {\\n  return sessions.at(0)!;\\n}\\n\\n// Do: strengthen the input; the assertion disappears\\nfunction newestSession(sessions: NonEmpty<Session>): Session {\\n  return sessions[0];\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWeakening the result to `Session | undefined` is the other total signature.\\n\\n## `unknown` over `any`\\n\\nExternal data is always `unknown`. Narrow before use.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't\\nfunction handle(input: any) {\\n  return input.foo.bar;\\n}\\n\\n// Do\\nfunction handle(input: unknown) {\\n  if (typeof input === \\\"object\\\" && input !== null && \\\"foo\\\" in input) {\\n    // narrowed; compiler verifies access\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\nExternal sources include RPC payloads, `JSON.parse`, `postMessage`, IPC, file contents, environment variables, database results.\\n\\n## Schemas before hand-rolled guards\\n\\nBefore writing a property-by-property type guard for external data, look for the repository's runtime schema library and existing schemas. Let one schema own validation and derive the TypeScript type from it. Do not maintain a schema, a duplicate interface, and a guard that can drift apart.\\n\\n```ts\\nimport { z } from \\\"zod\\\";\\n\\nconst UserSchema = z.object({\\n  id: z.string().uuid(),\\n  role: z.enum([\\\"admin\\\", \\\"member\\\"]),\\n});\\n\\ntype User = z.infer<typeof UserSchema>;\\n\\nfunction parseUser(input: unknown): User {\\n  return UserSchema.parse(input);\\n}\\n```\\n\\nUse `safeParse` when failure is an expected branch. Use the equivalent inference helper when the repository uses another schema library. Do not add a new schema dependency for one guard. This rule prefers the schema system the codebase already trusts.\\n\\n## No `as` casts\\n\\nEvery `as` is a potential runtime crash. Cast only after the type system has verified the claim.\\n\\n```ts\\nimport { z } from \\\"zod\\\";\\n\\n// Don't\\nconst user = data as User;\\n\\n// Don't\\nfunction isUser(data: unknown): data is User {\\n  return typeof data === \\\"object\\\" && data !== null && \\\"id\\\" in data;\\n}\\n\\n// Do\\nconst userSchema = z.object({ id: z.string(), name: z.string() });\\ntype User = z.infer<typeof userSchema>;\\n\\nfunction parseUser(data: unknown): User {\\n  return userSchema.parse(data);\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWhen the type comes first, annotate the validator with the type it proves. The compiler then rejects a validator that proves less than the type. Remove `name` from the object below and the assignment fails to compile.\\n\\n```ts\\ntype User = { id: string; name: string };\\n\\nconst userSchema: z.ZodType<User> = z.object({ id: z.string(), name: z.string() });\\n```\\n\\nWhen refactoring an `as` out of existing code, identify why TypeScript can't infer:\\n\\n- Missing discriminant: add one, switch to a discriminated union.\\n- Overly wide source type (e.g. `Record<string, unknown>`): narrow it.\\n- Untyped boundary: parse with the schema that owns the shape. Add a schema only where none exists.\\n- Genuinely inexpressible: use a branded type or `satisfies`.\\n\\n## Narrowing hierarchy\\n\\nFrom best to last-resort:\\n\\n1. **Discriminated union switch / if.** Compiler narrows automatically.\\n2. **`in` operator.** `\\\"key\\\" in obj` narrows to variants containing that key.\\n3. **`typeof` / `instanceof`.** For primitives and class instances.\\n4. **User-defined type guard.** When the above aren't enough.\\n5. **`as` cast.** Only after validation.\\n\\n```ts\\nfunction area(s: Shape): number {\\n  if (\\\"radius\\\" in s) return Math.PI * s.radius ** 2; // narrowed to circle\\n  return s.width * s.height; // narrowed to rect\\n}\\n```\\n\\n## Type guards\\n\\nA guard must actually verify the claim. A lying guard is worse than `as`.\\n\\n```ts\\nfunction isCircle(s: Shape): s is Shape & { kind: \\\"circle\\\" } {\\n  return s.kind === \\\"circle\\\";\\n}\\n```\\n\\nPrefer discriminant narrowing when possible.\\n\\n## Exhaustiveness\\n\\nIn default arms, assign the discriminant to a `never`-typed local.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Value-returning switch\\nfunction area(s: Shape): number {\\n  switch (s.kind) {\\n    case \\\"circle\\\":\\n      return Math.PI * s.radius ** 2;\\n    case \\\"rect\\\":\\n      return s.width * s.height;\\n    default: {\\n      const _exhaustive: never = s;\\n      return _exhaustive;\\n    }\\n  }\\n}\\n\\n// Void switch\\nfunction handle(s: Shape): void {\\n  switch (s.kind) {\\n    case \\\"circle\\\":\\n      drawCircle(s);\\n      break;\\n    case \\\"rect\\\":\\n      drawRect(s);\\n      break;\\n    default: {\\n      const _exhaustive: never = s;\\n      void _exhaustive;\\n    }\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\nReturn-style in value-returning switches, void-style in statement switches.\\n\\n## `satisfies` over `as`\\n\\n`satisfies` validates without widening literal types.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Widens, loses literal types.\\nconst config = { theme: \\\"dark\\\", cols: 3 } as Config;\\n\\n// Do. Validates AND preserves literal types.\\nconst config = { theme: \\\"dark\\\", cols: 3 } satisfies Config;\\n// config.theme is \\\"dark\\\" (literal), not string\\n```\\n\\n## Boundary validation\\n\\nValidate once where data crosses in. Trust types inside. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill.\\n\\n- **Wire formats** (proto, JSON-RPC): parse with `ignoreUnknownFields` so forward-compatible changes don't break old clients.\\n- **Persisted JSON:** versioned blob with a try/catch around the parse.\\n- **Don't re-validate** deep in call chains.\\n\\n## Schema-derived types\\n\\nWhen a `.proto`, OpenAPI spec, GraphQL schema, or database migration already defines a shape, derive from the generated types instead of duplicating them.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Duplicate shape, drifts when the schema changes.\\ntype CheckSummary = {\\n  totalCount: number;\\n  checks: { name: string; status: string }[];\\n};\\nfunction renderChecks(s: CheckSummary) {\\n  /* ... */\\n}\\n\\n// Do. Derive from the generated schema type.\\nimport type { ChecksMessage } from \\\"<generated module>\\\";\\nfunction renderChecks(s: Pick<ChecksMessage, \\\"totalCount\\\" | \\\"checks\\\">) {\\n  /* ... */\\n}\\n```\\n\\nReach for `Pick`, `Omit`, `Parameters`, `ReturnType`, `Awaited`, `typeof` before writing a new interface.\\n\\n## Object args\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Swap two args, still compiles.\\nopenFile(uri, {\\n  startLineNumber: 10,\\n  startColumn: 1,\\n  endLineNumber: 10,\\n  endColumn: 1,\\n});\\n\\n// Do. Order-independent, self-documenting.\\nopenFile({\\n  uri,\\n  selection: {\\n    startLineNumber: 10,\\n    startColumn: 1,\\n    endLineNumber: 10,\\n    endColumn: 1,\\n  },\\n});\\n```\\n\\nSkip on hot paths: per-frame render, tokenizers, parsers, anything in a tight loop where the allocation cost matters.\\n---\\nname: principle-type-system-discipline\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when designing types, reviewing a function signature, or writing code in any statically-typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand semantic primitives, parse external data at boundaries, refuse to lie to the compiler, exhaust variants, derive from authoritative schemas.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Type System Discipline\\n\\nThe type checker is a proof assistant. Use it to eliminate impossible states, mismatched primitives, and unhandled variants at compile time. A case the types let you ignore becomes a runtime failure the compiler could have stopped. Prefer defining errors and special cases out of existence over proliferating handlers. Unrepresentable states, total functions, and interface redesign (the patterns below) are the tools.\\n\\nApplies to any typed language. Skills like `typescript-best-practices` ground it in specific syntax.\\n\\n**The patterns:**\\n\\n- **Make illegal states unrepresentable.** Model variants as sum types: discriminated unions in TypeScript, enums with payloads in Rust/Swift/Kotlin, sealed classes in Scala, ADTs in Haskell/OCaml. Don't model state as a bag of optional fields where contradictory combinations compile. A subtle anti-pattern: `{ completed: boolean; completedAt?: Date }` admits `completed: true; completedAt: undefined`, which is meaningless. Derive the boolean from a single source like `completedAt !== null`, or model the variants explicitly as `{ kind: 'open' } | { kind: 'done'; at: Date }`. If a bug forces the question \\\"wait, can this combination actually happen?\\\", the type is too loose.\\n- **Types are constructions, not restrictions.** Build the type up from the values you want instead of carving them out of a looser type with checks. The invariant that seems to need a refinement type is usually a construction away. A non-empty list is a head plus a rest, not a list with a length check. A valid time range is a start plus a duration, not two timestamps you must keep ordered. No representation is privileged. A list of pairs is an even-length list if you interpret it that way, so choose the shape that cannot build the illegal value and expose the interface callers need on top.\\n- **Brand semantic primitives.** `UserId` and `OrderId` are strings underneath but should not be interchangeable. Newtypes in Rust, opaque types in Swift, value classes in Kotlin, phantom types in Haskell, branded intersections in TypeScript. Validate once at creation, trust the type downstream.\\n- **External data is untyped until parsed.** RPC payloads, JSON, IPC messages, CLI args, config files, environment variables, database rows. Have a parse function at every boundary that turns unstructured input into the typed model. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill for where to put validation.\\n- **Don't lie to the type system.** Casts, unsafe coercions, and assertion functions that bypass the compiler are latent runtime crashes. If the compiler can't prove a fact, prove it (validate, narrow, refine the model) or accept that the cast is a hazard.\\n- **Exhaustive matching is the compiler's job.** When you match on a sum type, the compiler must fail compilation if a new variant is added without handling. Use the idiom your language provides: `never`-typed binding in TypeScript, unannotated `match` in Rust, `-Wincomplete-patterns` in Haskell, sealed-class match exhaustiveness in Kotlin.\\n- **Derive types from authoritative schemas.** When a protocol buffer, OpenAPI spec, GraphQL schema, database migration, or design-system token file defines a shape, derive from it instead of hand-rolling a parallel type. See the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill.\\n- **Strengthen a type only where partiality appears.** A runtime assertion, null check, or \\\"this should never happen\\\" throw marks the place a type is too weak. Push that check up into the type. Then stop. The type system's job is to track the cases each use site must handle, not to describe the data as precisely as possible. Prefer total functions. `sum` of an empty list is 0, so it takes the plain list. `head` of an empty list has no answer, so it demands the non-empty one.\\n\\n**The tests:**\\n\\n- \\\"Can I write a comment explaining when this combination of fields is valid?\\\" If yes, the type is too loose. Split it into a sum type.\\n- \\\"Do two of my function arguments share a primitive type but mean different things?\\\" Brand them.\\n- \\\"Where did this `any`, this `as`, this `assertNotNull` come from?\\\" Trace it to the boundary and validate there instead.\\n- \\\"If a new variant is added next month, will the compiler tell the next agent where to add a case?\\\" If no, the match isn't exhaustive.\\n- \\\"Is this type duplicating a shape another file owns?\\\" Derive instead.\\n- \\\"Am I strengthening this type to keep an operation total, or just to be more precise?\\\" If nothing would otherwise panic, keep the plain type.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and limits\\n\\nFollow [host capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, authorized edits and compiler execution. The metadata above preserves the source invocation policy; do not assume the host enforces it.\\n\\nApply these patterns within the authorized task and exposed tools. If source access, writes or compiler execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal and state the missing checks. Identify the actual language limit when an invariant cannot be expressed in its types, and keep the required runtime validation explicit. Do not claim a compiler check ran without observed execution. Missing tools do not authorize installation or external actions.\\n---\\nname: principle-boundary-discipline\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Concentrate guards at system boundaries (CLI, config, network, external APIs); trust internal types and keep business logic in pure functions.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Boundary Discipline\\n\\nPlace validation, type narrowing, and error handling at system boundaries. Trust internal code unconditionally. Business logic lives in pure functions. The shell is thin and mechanical.\\n\\n**Why:** Scattered validation is noisy, redundant, and gives a false sense of safety. Keep logic out of framework wiring so it can be tested without the framework.\\n\\n**The pattern:**\\n- **At boundaries** (CLI args, config files, external APIs, network protocols): validate, return errors, handle defensively.\\n- **Inside the system:** typed data, error propagation, no re-validation. Trust the types.\\n- **Across the boundary.** Expose domain concepts, not the boundary's private representation. Keep general-purpose mechanism inside and special-purpose policy at the edge.\\n\\n**Applications:**\\n\\nValidation and error handling:\\n- Validate config at parse time (the boundary), not inside business logic\\n- Parse raw data into domain types at the boundary\\n- Do not re-export transport, storage, framework, or wire types through the public surface\\n- No redundant nil checks deep in call chains if the boundary already validated\\n\\nCode organization:\\n- Business logic in pure functions with no framework dependencies\\n- Parse functions: pure transforms from raw bytes to typed state\\n- Prompt construction: structured state in, string out\\n- Scoring and assessment: pure transforms from state to results\\n\\n**The tests:**\\n- \\\"Is this data crossing a system boundary right now?\\\" If not, validation is redundant.\\n- \\\"Can this be a pure function that the shell just calls?\\\" If yes, extract it.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nUse [the capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check available source access, writes and execution. Preserve user authorization and host permissions. If writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal and state which edits or checks were not performed. If required source or input contracts are missing, identify the missing inputs and keep the proposal provisional. Claim implementation and verification only from observed results.\\n\\nPreserve the explicit-invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: unslop\\ndescription: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Unslop\\n\\nEdit text to remove AI patterns.\\n\\n## Process\\n\\n1. Scan for the patterns below.\\n2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.\\n\\n## Patterns to detect and fix\\n\\nRule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.\\n\\n### Content\\n\\n3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** \\\"highlighting...\\\", \\\"ensuring...\\\", \\\"reflecting...\\\", \\\"showcasing...\\\", \\\"fostering...\\\". Delete or expand with real sources.\\n5. **Vague attributions.** \\\"Experts believe\\\", \\\"Industry reports suggest\\\", \\\"Some critics argue\\\". Name the source or delete.\\n\\n### Language\\n\\n7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.\\n8. **Fancy ways to say \\\"is\\\".** \\\"serves as\\\", \\\"stands as\\\", \\\"boasts\\\", \\\"features\\\". Just say \\\"is\\\" or \\\"has\\\".\\n9. **\\\"Not just X, but Y.\\\"** State the point directly instead.\\n10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.\\n11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.\\n12. **False ranges.** \\\"from X to Y\\\" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.\\n\\n### Style\\n\\n13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.\\n14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. \\\"If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions\\\" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. \\\"Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English.\\\" Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.\\n15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.\\n16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: \\\"**Performance:** Performance improved...\\\". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail (\\\"**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.\\\") is fine, not a tell.\\n17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.\\n18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.\\n19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.\\n\\n### Communication artifacts\\n\\n20. **Chatbot phrases.** \\\"I hope this helps!\\\", \\\"Let me know if...\\\", \\\"Of course!\\\", \\\"Certainly!\\\", \\\"Found the smoking gun!\\\" Remove.\\n22. **Sycophantic tone.** \\\"Great question! You're absolutely right!\\\" Respond directly.\\n\\n### Filler\\n\\n23. **Filler phrases.** \\\"In order to\\\" becomes \\\"To\\\". \\\"Due to the fact that\\\" becomes \\\"Because\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" gets deleted.\\n24. **Excessive hedging.** \\\"could potentially possibly be argued that it might\\\" becomes \\\"may\\\".\\n25. **Generic conclusions.** \\\"The future looks bright.\\\" State specific plans or facts.\\n\\n### Jargon\\n\\n26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in \\\"API surface\\\"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. \\\"Substrate\\\" becomes \\\"base\\\". \\\"Wedge in\\\" becomes \\\"add\\\". \\\"Vector\\\" becomes \\\"way\\\" or \\\"method\\\". \\\"Gold-plating\\\" becomes \\\"more than the job needs\\\". \\\"Ratchet\\\" becomes the mechanism's real name or \\\"a limit that only tightens\\\". \\\"Evacuate\\\" becomes \\\"move out\\\". \\\"Endgame\\\" becomes \\\"the last phase\\\". Pick the concrete word.\\n\\n### Plain speech\\n\\n27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** \\\"the database stays close at hand\\\", \\\"SQL you can read\\\", \\\"types that follow your schema\\\" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: \\\"`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database\\\", \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.\\n28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.\\n29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch \\\"is/are/was/were + past participle\\\" and name the actor: \\\"queries are validated\\\" becomes \\\"the compiler validates queries\\\", \\\"the file is parsed by the loader\\\" becomes \\\"the loader parses the file\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.\\n30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** \\\"runs quickly\\\" becomes \\\"is fast\\\" or the number. \\\"significantly improves\\\" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.\\n31. **Prefer the plain word.** \\\"utilize\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"leverage\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"facilitate\\\" becomes \\\"help\\\", \\\"numerous\\\" becomes \\\"many\\\", \\\"in the event that\\\" becomes \\\"if\\\". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.\\n32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms (\\\"wire it or delete it\\\"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code (\\\"the plan holds it\\\"), figurative verbs (\\\"rides along\\\", \\\"stands on\\\"), stock framing phrases. \\\"A dial worth turning\\\" becomes \\\"a parameter worth varying\\\". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.\\n33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. \\\"Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write\\\" becomes \\\"The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing.\\\" Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.\\n---\\nname: how\\ndescription: \\\"Use for \\\\\\\"how does X work\\\\\\\", code walkthroughs before changing something, and placement / ownership / layering questions (\\\\\\\"where should this live\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"which package owns this\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"is this the right layer\\\\\\\"). Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# How\\n\\nExplore the codebase to answer \\\"how does X work?\\\" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.\\n\\nBefore dispatch, read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Bind worker operations to tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Request the inherited parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Retain rejections and disclose replacements; an unavailable required selection is blocked. Record requested and observed selections separately, using unknown for unobserved values.\\n\\nKeep explorers and explainers read-only. Worker assignments record their role, distinct angle, exact input revision, identity and inspected results under the assignment contract. Reading a prompt does not register a worker. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and perform the same exploration and explanation roles sequentially. Never claim parallel or independent exploration from sequential work. If source access is unavailable or denied, report the gap and limit the explanation to inspected evidence.\\n\\n## Step 1. Assess Complexity\\n\\nIf the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.\\n\\n- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as \\\"how does function X work\\\"): no explorers. One explainer explores and explains in a single pass. Go to Step 2b.\\n- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): use parallel explorers when available, then hand off to the explainer. Go to Step 2a.\\n\\nWhen in doubt, take the simple path.\\n\\n## Step 2a. Explore (complex questions only)\\n\\nDecompose the question into 2 to 4 exploration angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Dispatch all explorers together through an observed worker binding, with distinct assignments:\\n\\n- Role: how explorer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nEach explorer gets the prompt in `references/explorer-prompt.md` with its angle filled in. If workers are unavailable, explore those distinct angles sequentially and retain separate findings. Then go to Step 3.\\n\\n## Step 2b. Direct Explain (simple questions)\\n\\nAssign one explainer worker that explores and explains in one pass:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. The explainer reads and traces the source itself. If workers are unavailable, apply this role sequentially. Go to Step 4.\\n\\n## Step 3. Synthesize (complex questions only)\\n\\nOnce all explorer results have been inspected, assign one explainer worker to synthesize their findings into one explanation:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` with every explorer's findings filled in. In reduced capability, synthesize the sequential findings under the same read-only role.\\n\\n## Step 4. Present\\n\\nPresent the explainer's output to the user. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nThe explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.\\n# Explorer Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild each explorer worker's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are exploring a codebase to understand how something works. Gather facts. Trace code paths, read implementations, map components. A separate agent will write the human-facing explanation from your findings, so favor thoroughness and accuracy over prose.\\n\\nWhen independent workers are available, other explorers investigate different slices in parallel. In reduced capability, the coordinator explores distinct slices sequentially and discloses that limit. Don't try to cover everything. Focus on your assigned angle and go deep.\\n\\n## Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## Your Exploration Angle\\n\\n{EXPLORATION_ANGLE}\\n\\n## Exploration Instructions\\n\\nStart by finding the relevant code. Use available file discovery, symbol search and file-reading tools to understand the actual implementation. Don't guess from names. Read the code.\\n\\nFollow this pattern:\\n1. **Find the entry point.** What triggers this behavior? A user action, an API call, a scheduled job? Find where it starts.\\n2. **Trace the flow.** Follow the call chain from the entry point. Read each function. Understand what data flows through and how it transforms.\\n3. **Map the key abstractions.** What types, interfaces, services, or classes are central? Read their definitions. Understand what they represent and why they exist.\\n4. **Find the boundaries.** Where does this subsystem interface with others? What goes in, what comes out?\\n5. **Look for the non-obvious.** Anything surprising? Anything that looks like a historical artifact? Anything a newcomer would misunderstand?\\n\\nKeep exploring until you can describe the full picture without hand-waving. If you hit a part you can't trace, say so explicitly. \\\"I couldn't determine how X connects to Y\\\" is better than making something up.\\n\\n## Output\\n\\nReturn your findings in this structure. Be factual and specific. Reference exact file paths, function names, type names, and line numbers where relevant.\\n\\n### Components Found\\nThe key types, services, classes, and abstractions. For each: name, file path, and a one-sentence description of what it does.\\n\\n### Flow\\nThe execution flow step by step. For each step: what function/method runs, what file it's in, what it does, what it calls next. Include the data that flows between steps.\\n\\n### Files Read\\nEvery file you read during exploration, so the explainer can reference them.\\n\\n### Boundaries\\nWhere this subsystem connects to other parts of the codebase. The inputs and outputs.\\n\\n### Non-Obvious Things\\nAnything surprising, historically motivated, or easy to get wrong. Things that look like they should work one way but work another.\\n\\n### Open Questions\\nAnything you couldn't fully trace or understand. Be honest about gaps.\\n# Explainer Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild the explainer worker's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are writing an architectural explanation for a senior engineer. For complex questions, explorers have traced different slices and gathered findings, independently when supported or sequentially with an explicit reduced-capability disclosure. For simple questions, omit Explorer Findings and explore the source yourself in this role. Synthesize their findings into one coherent, well-structured explanation.\\n\\n## Original Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## Explorer Findings\\n\\n{EXPLORER_FINDINGS_ALL}\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\nThe explorers each investigated a different angle of the same subsystem. Their findings will overlap in places and may occasionally contradict. Reconcile them. Merge overlapping descriptions, resolve contradictions by checking the code yourself, and combine the separate slices into a unified picture.\\n\\nWrite an explanation a senior engineer unfamiliar with this area could read and walk away with a solid mental model, understanding the architecture well enough to start working in it confidently.\\n\\nYou have read-only access to the codebase to check anything, clarify a detail, or fill a gap. Use available file reading, symbol search and file discovery as needed. When explorer findings are supplied, you should not need to re-explore from scratch. Without them, trace entry points, calls, data, abstractions and boundaries yourself; disclose anything you cannot trace.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nUse this structure, adapted to what makes sense for the question. Not every section is needed for every question.\\n\\n### Overview\\n1-2 paragraphs. What is this thing, what does it do, why does it exist. Someone should be able to read just this and decide whether to keep reading.\\n\\n### Key Concepts\\nThe important types, services, or abstractions needed to follow the rest. Brief definitions, not exhaustive.\\n\\n### How It Works\\nThe core of the explanation, and the longest section. Walk through the flow: what triggers it, what happens step by step, where data goes, what the decision points are.\\n\\nUse prose, not pseudocode. Reference specific files and functions so the reader knows where to look, but don't dump large code blocks unless a snippet is essential to a point.\\n\\nWhen the flow involves multiple components talking to each other, or data transforming through stages, include a diagram. Use mermaid (```mermaid) for structured flows (sequence diagrams, flowcharts, component graphs) or ASCII art for simpler relationships where mermaid would be overkill. Use your judgment. A diagram should clarify, not decorate. If prose covers the flow, skip the diagram.\\n\\n### Where Things Live\\nA brief file/directory map. Just the ones someone would need to start working here.\\n\\n### Gotchas\\nNon-obvious things, surprising behavior, historical context, pitfalls. Skip this section if there's nothing worth calling out.\\n\\n## Communication Style\\n\\n- Use concrete language, not abstractions-about-abstractions\\n- Say \\\"the `UserService` calls `AuthClient.refresh()`\\\" not \\\"the service delegates to the client\\\"\\n- When something is complex, explain why it's complex. Don't just describe the complexity\\n- When something is simple, don't pad it out\\n- If there's a helpful analogy, use it. If there isn't, don't force one\\n- If the explorers flagged open questions or gaps, acknowledge them rather than hiding them\\n\"}},\"tasks\":[{\"caseId\":\"trace-provenance\",\"identity\":{\"chunk_id\":\"8494bf\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000006083,\"exit_code\":1,\"original_token_count\":11,\"output\":\"58d7f1d7b5659a283cce0daf0661a4b2abc53a67\\n\"},\"task\":{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat README.md task-contract.json\",\"workdir\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/trace-provenance\",\"max_output_tokens\":10000},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"c9fe0d\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000004916,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":1896,\"output\":\"# Synthetic task inputs\\nThis repository identifies inert workflow inputs and current case contracts.
